--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed from the graph so that the two black nodes (B and M) are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the paths between these two nodes and find the critical nodes whose removal will break all possible paths.

1. **Identify the black nodes:** The black nodes are B and M.
2. **Identify the immediate neighbors of B and M:**
   - B is connected to N, H, and G.
   - M is connected to L, F, and K.
3. **Trace the paths from B to M:**
   - From B to H to G to F to M
   - From B to H to G to X to F to M
   - From B to N to C to I to J to D to P to O
   - From B to N to C to I to J to F to M
   - From B to N to C to I to P to O

4. **Identify the critical nodes:**
   - Removing H will disconnect the path B-H-G-F-M and B-H-G-X-F-M.
   - Removing G will also disconnect the path B-H-G-F-M and B-H-G-X-F-M.
   - Removing C will disconnect the paths involving C (B-N-C-I-J-D-P-O, B-N-C-I-J-F-M, B-N-C-I-P-O).
   - Removing I will disconnect the paths involving I (B-N-C-I-J-D-P-O, B-N-C-I-J-F-M, B-N-C-I-P-O).

Given that removing either C or I will disconnect multiple paths, we need to check if removing both is necessary. Since removing C alone does not disconnect all paths (e.g., B-H-G-F-M and B-H-G-X-F-M), we need to remove at least one more node to ensure complete disconnection.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes to remove is 2, and the cut set can be [C, G].

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [C, G]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': ['J', 'E']}

   - N is connected to Y, O, B, and C.
7. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "Y":**
   - Y is connected to N and O.
8. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "O":**
   - O is connected to N, Y, C, and P.
9. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "C":**
   - C is connected to O, N, P, and I.
10. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "P":**
    - P is connected to O, C, and I.
11. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "I":**
    - I is connected to C and P.
12. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "H":**
    - H is connected to B, G, and K.
13. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "G":**
    - G is connected to H and K.
14. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "K":**
    - K is connected to F, X, G, and H.
15. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "X":**
    - X is connected to F and K.
16. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "J":**
    - J is connected to D and P.
17. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of "D":**
    - D is connected to J.

Now, trace the paths from L to J:
- L -> E -> B -> N -> O -> P -> J

This path has a length of 5.

Let's verify if there is a shorter path:
- L -> E -> B -> N -> O -> C -> P -> J: Length 6
- L -> E -> F -> K -> H -> B -> N -> O -> P -> J: Length 8
- Other paths are longer or involve revisiting nodes without reducing the path length.

Thus, the shortest path is:

Length: 5, Path: [L, E, B, N, O, P, J]

### Identifying Paths Between \( \mathbf{L} \) and \( \mathbf{J} \):

1. **Direct Route from \( \mathbf{J} \):**
   - \( \mathbf{J} \to \mathbf{P} \to \mathbf{I} \to \mathbf{H} \to \mathbf{G} \to \mathbf{E} \to \mathbf{L} \)

   - \( \mathbf{J} \to \mathbf{P} \to \mathbf{I} \to \mathbf{K} \to \mathbf{F} \to \mathbf{X} \to \mathbf{G} \to \mathbf{E} \to \mathbf{L} \)

2. **Alternate Routes involving other nodes:**

   There are many overlapping paths, so we need to ensure we cover all necessary connections:
   - \( \mathbf{J} \to \mathbf{P} \to \mathbf{I} \to \mathbf{H} \to \mathbf{X} \to \mathbf{F} \to \mathbf{M} \to \mathbf{E} \to \mathbf{L} \)
   - \( \mathbf{J} \to \mathbf{P} \to \mathbf{H} \to \mathbf{X} \to \mathbf{F} \to \mathbf{M} \to \mathbf{E} \to \mathbf{L} \)
   - \( \mathbf{J} \to \mathbf{P} \to \mathbf{I} \to \mathbf{O} \to \mathbf{C} \to \mathbf{B} \to \mathbf{E} \to \mathbf{L} \)

### Observing Bottlenecks in Paths:

- It’s clear that the nodes X, E, H, and P are critical bottlenecks in many paths since removing an edge connected to any of these nodes would disrupt several paths connecting \( \mathbf{L} \) and \( \mathbf{J} \).

### Minimum Edge Removal Calculation:

By finding paths and removing edges one by one to check which would be the minimal set of edges to remove to fully disconnect \( \mathbf{L} \) and \( \mathbf{J} \):

1. **Removing edge** \( \mathbf{(E, L)} \) or any direct edge connecting to \( \mathbf{L} \) or \( \mathbf{J} \) first:

   After removing this edge, \( \mathbf{L} \) is disconnected from \( \mathbf{E} \). However, paths still exist through \( \mathbf{J} \to \mathbf{P} \to \mathbf{I} \to \mathbf{H} \to \mathbf{G} \to \mathbf{X} \to \mathbf{M} \to \mathbf{F} \to \mathbf{E} \).

2. **Removing edge** \( \mathbf{(J, P)} \):

   After removing \( \mathbf{(J, P)} \), \( \mathbf{J} \) is disconnected from \( \mathbf{P} \).

3. **Ensuring Final Disconnection:**

   We need one more edge to completely disconnect \( \mathbf{L} \) from \( \mathbf{J} \):

   - \( \mathbf{(E, G)} \):

### Final Edges to Remove:

To ensure complete disconnection with the minimum edges removed:
  - Edges to remove: 3
  - Cut Set: \( [(\mathbf{E, L}), (\mathbf{J, P}), (\mathbf{E, G})] \)
